## November 1, 2026 — P3 read-only readiness review complete; combined package NO-GO

**Authority and preservation.** Completed only the shared-handoff-authorized read-only P3 readiness review in `C:\Users\Dev\CRM-P2-20261101` as `DESKTOP-UH85JC0\Dev`, on `codex/crm-option-a-p1-20261029` at local/live remote `5025b495e30a1ea46f300c1f3bec61c8db64ada4`. The workspace was clean; source commit `4d8fa3f81d2ac9db54e6b3ba61b4800cc057f8b8` remained unchanged beneath documentation-only commits. The protected original checkout remained untouched at `295845912f5a4fefe56464b490dffd5ab502e7a3`, with 327 displayed / 326 staged paths. No build, test, application, database, Graph, source edit, package, merge or deployment occurred.

**Existing contract and smallest design.** P2 already provides `AuditContext`, canonical typed before/after serialization and `CRMTechnicalAuditWriter.WriteDataChange(connection, transaction, ...)`. Its focused fictional tests prove business row + audit row commit together, caller rollback removes both, serializer/audit failure rolls back business work, and retry does not duplicate. `SecurityAuditEvent` already exists from schema v21 and current schema is v24, so the smallest P3 design requires **no schema migration**: capture one P2 context per user operation; collect exact changed entities while using the repository's existing connection/transaction; call the writer exactly once before that transaction's existing commit; let any capture/serialization/insert error abort the whole business transaction; and retain the existing `Activity` history as the user-facing business timeline while `SecurityAuditEvent` remains the technical immutable change record. Empty/no-op operations must not emit an event. Restore/version compatibility follows from the existing table, backup and migration chain; migration/backup tests should explicitly prove audit rows survive upgrade/copy and failed migrations leave both schema and audit state intact.

**Candidate components and scope risk.** Primary CRM candidates are `Form1.vb` (return and pass, rather than discard, the P2 context), `Data/CRMRepository.vb`, `Data/CRMRepository.Maintenance.vb`, `Data/CRMTechnicalAudit.vb`, `tests/CRM.Data.Tests/CRMTechnicalAuditTests.vb`, and `tests/CRM.Data.Tests/CRMDatabaseTests.vb`. If the policy is every persistent CRM write, `Data/CRMBaselineImporter.vb` and `Data/CRMPostalCodeImporter.vb` are also in scope because they own independent transactions. The repository has batch/operation transactions plus several direct transactions, document filesystem compensation, maintenance/restore/merge paths and existing `Activity` writes; a single commit hook alone cannot reconstruct accurate before/after values. The unresolved policy boundary is whether initialization/import/geocode and document/filesystem side effects require technical events or only interactive business mutations. That decision must precede an exact implementation file list.

**GoGreen participation.** GoGreen links the shared audit contract but does not pass its P2 context into `DraftBatchProcessor` or `CrmFollowUpRecorder`. `CrmFollowUpRecorder` independently writes `Activity`, recipient interaction dates and `MonthlyDraftFollowUp` in one CRM transaction; `DraftAuditRepository` then records completion/pending state in a separate GoGreen SQLite database, after a Graph draft already exists. P3 can make the CRM technical event atomic with those CRM changes by passing the same context/correlation into `EnsureRecorded` and writing before its CRM commit. It cannot make Graph plus two SQLite databases one atomic transaction. Preserve the existing idempotent marker/retry model, use the shared correlation/Graph message ID to reconcile, and keep a failed GoGreen marker update as pending/recoverable rather than rolling back an already verified Graph draft. Candidate GoGreen files are `MainForm.vb`, `DraftBatchProcessor.vb`, `ICrmFollowUpRecorder.vb`, `Data/CrmFollowUpRecorder.vb`, `DraftAuditRepository.vb`, and focused `DraftBatchProcessorTests.vb`, `CrmIntegrationTests.vb`, and `CRMTechnicalAuditLinkTests.vb`.

**Minimal sequence, evidence gaps and estimate.** Separately authorize: (1) decide the mutation/audit coverage boundary and event granularity; (2) add CRM context/change propagation and atomic writer calls, beginning with a narrow interactive slice; (3) add fictional commit/rollback/audit-failure/no-op/batch/restore tests and migration/backup preservation checks; (4) add GoGreen correlation plus CRM-atomic audit and retry/reconciliation tests; (5) only then assemble an isolated combined test package. Static evidence is strong for the writer and existing GoGreen retry behavior, but there is no integrated repository audit test, no exhaustive mutation inventory decision, no audit-preservation assertion for backup/upgrade, and no combined CRM/GoGreen package evidence. Estimated engineering effort is **12–20 focused hours**, with high uncertainty until the coverage decision; document/filesystem compensation and all-import coverage can increase it.

**Decision / stop.** **NO-GO** for a responsible combined CRM + GoGreen isolated test package by 10 p.m. November 1. At review time approximately 2 hours 38 minutes remained, implementation was not authorized, and the coverage decision plus integrated tests were unresolved. This is not a claim that P3 is infeasible; it is a scope/evidence decision against rushing a package. Work-computer testing tomorrow requires separate implementation/package authorization, a frozen file/scope list, clean isolated Dev workspaces at verified remote heads, fictional copied databases only, passing focused automated gates, package hashes/instructions, rollback/reconciliation instructions, and explicit confirmation that live Graph remains deferred. No deadline or package is promised. **STOP for Rob/Priya review; no P3 implementation or live Graph work is authorized.**

Existing untracked `output/`, `s2/` and shared `Test/` are preserved.

### Decision memo — recommend B, a bounded transactional version ledger

**Current conventions — source verification PASS; current database state NOT REVERIFIED.** References below are at `dd223e6` (unchanged application source). `Data/CRMRepository.vb:19-87` supports an owning operation or shared `BeginBatch` transaction; Form1 saves commit that batch (`Form1.vb:1595-1740`). `UpdateSite` (`906-974`) logs human-readable `SiteUpdated` in the same transaction, but also writes Site.UpdatedUtc and RecycleProgramProfile fields/timestamp. `AppendChange` (`1592` onward) trims and collapses NULL/empty text: it is not an exact typed version record. `InsertActivity` (`1525-1543`) can overwrite Site.DTAssociate for six activity types without retaining that field's old value. `SetLatestSiteActivity` (`1333-1368`) retains prior dated activities through supersession; its `ActivityCorrected` event handles clearing a date, not arbitrary entity correction. `Database/CRM_v16.sql:26-63` requires a Company/Site/Contact anchor and restricts event types. Associate has only ID/name/active state, and no normal rename/activation save API was found. `GoGreenMonthlyDrafts/RecycleManagement_v1.sql:90-106` defines assignment IDs/FKs/date-order check and one open assignment per Company + IFNULL(CustomerAccountId,0), not one per associate. `CRMRepository.Maintenance.vb:233-269` closes conflicting assignments and moves them during company merges in its transaction, but its summary does not preserve every old/new assignment value. `Database/CRM_v21.sql:105-145` security events describe identities/functions/outcomes, not entity versions; authentication remains unimplemented on this branch. Do not invent an authenticated Rob identity from the default Robert Nevins activity attribution.

Protected history is an established requirement, not a claim that current tables are technically immutable: correction links and original events are retained by normal date correction, while merge paths can reparent Activity and imported corrections can change attribution. There is no general complete entity-version mechanism. Neither arbitrary Activity anchors, date-correction events nor unstructured SecurityAuditEvent.Details provide truthful exact Associate/assignment history. The prior audit gate FAIL remains correct. Prior verified Chelsie/data-binding and lineage findings are reused, not rerun or upgraded to deployed-binary proof.

**A — external signed/hashed manifest plus before/after report: reject as sole ledger.** It minimizes product changes and is useful independent evidence and rollback documentation, but is not atomically committed with CRM rows, does not travel automatically with database backup/restore, cannot enforce normal future-save coverage, and can be separated from the database. A hash alone is not a signature or immutability. It therefore FAILS the existing in-database protected audit/version requirement unless Rob explicitly changes that requirement; no such waiver is inferred.

**B — recommended, proposed only.** Add three small tables restricted to four entity kinds: Associate, Site, CompanySalesAssignment and Activity (the latter is necessary for the four approved attribution corrections). Capture exact full before/after rows for version reconstruction and separately enumerable field deltas; do not create a general all-CRM audit framework. No existing business table or prior history is replaced. Proposed schema contract:

- `ChangeBatch`: `BatchId TEXT PRIMARY KEY` (UUID), `OperationKind TEXT NOT NULL` (NormalSave/Correction), `RecordedUtc TEXT NOT NULL` (actual UTC ISO-8601 Z), `ActorKind TEXT NOT NULL` (WindowsOperator/System/AuthenticatedAppUser), `ActorId TEXT NOT NULL`, `ActorDisplayName TEXT NOT NULL`, `WindowsUserName TEXT NOT NULL`, `ComputerName TEXT NOT NULL`, `Source TEXT NOT NULL` (component/action + exact build commit), `Reason TEXT NOT NULL`, `AuthorizationReference TEXT NULL`, `ManifestSha256 TEXT NULL`, `PlanSha256 TEXT NULL`, `SourceSnapshotSha256 TEXT NULL`, `SchemaVersion INTEGER NOT NULL`, `ExpectedVersions INTEGER NOT NULL`, `ExpectedFields INTEGER NOT NULL`. Correction requires authorization and all three hashes; normal saves leave them NULL. No password/token/IP collection or authentication feature is added. An operator-declared authorization reference is distinct from proven authentication.
- `EntityVersion`: `VersionId INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, `BatchId TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES ChangeBatch` (NO ACTION), `EntityType TEXT NOT NULL` (four-value CHECK), `EntityId INTEGER NOT NULL`, `Revision INTEGER NOT NULL` (>0), `PreviousVersionId INTEGER NULL REFERENCES EntityVersion` (NO ACTION), `Action TEXT NOT NULL` (Insert/Update/Delete), `BeforeRow TEXT NOT NULL`, `AfterRow TEXT NOT NULL` (canonical typed JSON), UNIQUE `(EntityType,EntityId,Revision)` and UNIQUE `(BatchId,EntityType,EntityId)`. Index `IX_EntityVersion_Batch(BatchId)`; the unique entity/revision index serves history lookup. First observation of an existing row is revision 1, with its exact before image and no predecessor: explicitly unknown earlier history, never fabricated backdated versions. Subsequent before images must equal the predecessor after image; mismatch fails closed. Entity keys are historical values, deliberately not cascading FKs to mutable business rows.
- `EntityFieldChange`: `VersionId INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES EntityVersion` (NO ACTION), `FieldName TEXT NOT NULL`, `OldType TEXT NOT NULL`, `OldValue BLOB NULL`, `NewType TEXT NOT NULL`, `NewValue BLOB NULL`; PRIMARY KEY `(VersionId,FieldName)`. Types are `absent/null/integer/real/text/blob`; absence describes a nonexistent row, not SQL NULL. Values use one versioned canonical codec: invariant signed decimal integers, round-trip real encoding, exact UTF-8 text and raw blobs; absent/null carry no payload. Row JSON uses the same type tags and base64 payload bytes, ordinal column order, and an explicit row-exists flag. No trim/case/date normalization; preserve empty strings, NULL, original offsets and original stored types. Inserts/deletes enumerate every column, including keys and NULL-valued columns; updates enumerate exactly changed fields. Full images must reconstruct the field deltas bidirectionally.

One shared transaction-bound writer captures before state under the write lock, applies existing business rules, captures actual after state and appends the batch/versions/fields before COMMIT. Multiple changes to the same row in one transaction coalesce to one net version; a no-op produces no version. Counts are finalized before the one batch INSERT, not updated afterward. Failed mutation, missing actor, stale predecessor, audit failure or coverage mismatch rolls back all business and ledger writes. Unique batch IDs plus a lookup of prior committed correction hash/plan prevent replay; a verified already-completed batch is a report/STOP, not a second correction. Reversal after commit must be a separately approved compensating version, never an edit/delete of old audit rows.

Append-only protection: no public ledger update/delete API; six narrow BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE rejection triggers on these three ledger tables only, with FK NO ACTION and verified schema at writer entry. These are explicit history-protection guards, not broad automatic business-data capture triggers. Application validation enforces predecessor identity/revision, exact field coverage and hashes; all supported writers must use it. SQLite file owners can still drop guards or rewrite the file, so this is NOT adversarial tamper-proof/WORM storage. Retain a separately protected signed postcommit report binding source/manifest/plan hashes, resulting database hash and ledger digest for independent tamper detection/recovery. Signature key ownership/storage must be approved before a data trial; do not fabricate a signature. If Rob requires protection against privileged file owners, this minimal design is insufficient: STOP for a separate trust-boundary decision, not an unapproved service/framework.

### Smallest implementation touchpoints and compatibility — proposed, NOT AUTHORIZED

- New `Database/CRM_v25.sql` plus `Data/CRMDatabase.vb` version 25 and migration inclusion in `CRM.vbproj`/data-test project. One transactional additive migration creates ledger/guards and records SchemaVersion/user_version 25 only after successful checks; it must not replay business corrections or invent baseline history. Preserve v1-v24 scripts. `CRMDatabase.vb:33-51,99-129,135-155` opens read/write/WAL, rejects versions above 24, applies sequential scripts and backs up upgrades. Existing Test22 CRM therefore cannot use schema 25; it must not be launched even for a compatibility probe against protected data. Rollback requires the matched old binary plus the exact pre-upgrade snapshot, not an old binary against a new database and not the older corrected reference. Current packaged binary remains incompatible / NOT VALIDATED for this proposal.
- New narrow `Data/CRMChangeLedger.vb` and `CRMRepository` correction/Associate/assignment APIs, using the same codec and transaction helper for both ordinary saves and corrections. Integrate Site creation/edit, display-address edit, company-prefix changes, activity-induced DT attribution, archive/restore, and maintenance merge paths for exact row versions. Preserve existing user-facing Activity events and normal timestamp rules; the correction API must be field-specific, not call broad UpdateSite (which would rewrite notes/timestamps). Include Activity insertion, supersession and reparenting paths so subsequent changes to a versioned Activity cannot silently break its chain. Preserve existing business behavior; no new edit screens are required.
- Other writers cannot be ignored: `Data/CRMBaselineImporter.vb:96-103` changes Site salesperson; `CRMPostalCodeImporter.vb:38` changes Site; dormant companion `RcLogImporter.vb:227-289` inserts associates/assignments/activities; `CompanyMaintenanceService.vb:64-70` moves sites/assignments and actually deletes a conflicting assignment. Their legacy routes must explicitly refuse schema-25 CRM writes until separately adapted, while retaining existing legacy-database behavior. They are not silently activated by this design. Active `CrmFollowUpRecorder.vb:27-96` opens CRM independently and writes Activity in its own transaction, bypassing CRMDatabase's version gate; share the narrow ledger helper via project linkage and add exact supported-version/ledger validation there. Do not change sending, eligibility, suppression or recipient behavior. Inventory all tracked writers again before implementation approval; an uncovered writer blocks acceptance. Old companion binaries also lack these gates: operationally exclude them from a schema-25 file. No claim that SQLite schema guards alone block old/direct business writers.
- New focused data/UI tests and read-only history retrieval by entity/key are necessary to prove reconstruction and normal-save coverage. No general audit browser, authentication implementation, trigger-based all-table framework, external integration or old-history backfill is recommended. `Data/CRMBackupService.vb` whole-database backup/restore should retain ledger with business rows; verify that property and supported-version/provenance checks in focused tests before approving restore with schema 25. Do not redesign backup now.

### Future correction procedure — proposal only, requires separate gates below

1. Reauthorize exact source, plan, approved implementation/build and target; require writer-closed, stable, ordinary-file snapshot and account for all WAL/SHM/journal files. Stop on nonempty/unknown companions or changing/locked source; do not ignore a needed WAL with immutable mode. Reverify frozen baseline/reference/manifest identities and source SHA-256. Last source `F6445EED...1378C` is historical evidence only. Original manifest SHA-256 is `8AA91C24DD1F70CF6DC737A94508BB811DDECDFAC8B2EEA942B9BFB6D3992E24`; preserve it unchanged. A separately reviewed, hashed derived execution plan must reference it and explicitly exclude Site 352. Pin all precise keys, exact typed old/new fields and complete inserted rows; no name-based matching or inferred IDs. No executable correction script exists from this design task.
2. Create/verify no-overwrite closed-source rollback and disposable candidate only under separately approved paths. Keep source/reference/recoveries immutable; never replace latest with `CRM_corrected.sqlite`. On the candidate, separately apply the authorized additive schema migration and prove every pre-existing business value unchanged. Preserve both the pre-schema-25 rollback and pre-correction schema-25 baseline. Record before/after schema identity separately from correction evidence.
3. On schema 25, enable and verify FK before a single non-deferred write transaction (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` equivalent). Recheck all 196 update/19 insert preconditions inside it; exact key + typed expected-old predicates and affected-row count 1 for each update, nonexistence for each insert. Abort the whole transaction on any mismatch, unexpected target field, duplicate batch or ledger drift. Record all 196 updates (220 field deltas) and 19 inserts (129 fields) as 215 entity versions / 349 field records and one batch if the verified plan is unchanged; no silent partial skip. The original 197th update / 221st field is excluded Site 352, which must remain Robert Nevins.
4. Insert approved Associate 21 Dan Hurd before dependent assignments; retain all historical associate/assignment IDs. Close exactly Jeff rows 1,12-14,34,67-68,70,80,84,91-92,96,107,112,123,132,136 at `2026-09-18`, then insert Dan replacements 146-163 effective `2026-09-18`, preserving each approved company/account/territory mapping and source-date spelling. Do not infer interval inclusivity beyond the approved dates; equality is permitted by the schema. Verify 18 closures/18 replacements, FK/date-order and unique open Company/account assignment invariants. Include assignment-owner rows 22/48/62/74/116/121/143, Associate canonical names/alias activation and all manifest-targeted Site fields. Preserve Chelsie Bonstein and Dan Hurd spelling. Activities 120/121/262/263 get only their approved AssociateId correction, with full original row images in the new ledger; do not rewrite occurrence/creation dates, summaries, supersession links or claim the correction happened in September.
5. Correction time belongs in ChangeBatch.RecordedUtc, not historical business timestamps. Preserve Site 200/285 newer data and timestamps, Activity 1404, Activity 651 supersession, Activities 1374-1404, Site 427, all 51 newer rows and every other post-baseline change. Do not call activity attribution or ordinary broad saves as reconciliation side effects. Leave Site 246 address, RecycleProgramProfile 274 Notes and ContactMethod 759 IsPrimary decisions untouched. No new ActivityCorrected/SiteUpdated rows are required for this one reconciliation: the typed ledger is its truthful history.
6. Before commit require full all-table key/value allowlist comparison against frozen pre-correction state, exact business deltas, complete ledger count/field/image/chain/hash coverage, integrity `ok`, zero FK failures and assignment/name invariants. Business population increases by 19 only; compute totals from the freshly verified baseline, plus one migration metadata row if counting across upgrade, plus actual ledger rows (proposed correction adds 565 ledger rows), not obsolete unconditional total 7,474. Precommit mismatch => full rollback. After commit close cleanly, independently reopen a consistent snapshot for the same checks and record physical hashes/sidecars separately. Postcommit mismatch cannot be SQL-rolled back: quarantine the disposable candidate, retain evidence and STOP; for a later expressly authorized Z-write, restoration of the verified exact preoperation snapshot must be included in that operation's approved rollback contract, never improvised or performed while writers run. Never promote a failing candidate.

Missing target creation is intentional; there is no current path-length preflight.

The existing local-C backup folder was length 226; final path 263 and `.creating` target 272. ACL/token failure remains possible but unsupported; missing/unavailable parent is contradicted; native SQLite/Windows path handling is part of the strongest path-length hypothesis. PID 5204's outer startup exception remains separate and unresolved.
- **ONE recommended correction — proposal only.** Centralize final/temporary path construction in `CRMBackupService` and enforce a documented legacy-compatible full-path ceiling of 259 characters (260 including the Windows terminator) on both names before directory creation or SQLite open. With the unchanged 36-character managed filename and nine-character `.creating` suffix, the temporary path is controlling and permits a normalized folder no longer than 213 characters. Throw an actionable `PathTooLongException` that reports actual/max lengths and tells the user to choose a shorter local backup folder; preserve the configured value and never silently redirect it. Expose/reuse the same validator from `SettingsForm` before its existing create/write probe, and revalidate in `CreateBackup` at point of use. Keep the filename pattern, `.creating` atomic staging, integrity validation, retention of ten, restore/merge behavior and SQLite connection modes unchanged. Invalid or unwritable destinations fail through the existing settings/manual/scheduled warning surfaces without changing CRM data; the existing cleanup remains responsible for any known temporary file. An overlong destination fails deterministically before SQLite and leaves both the configured folder value and existing backups untouched. The tradeoff is deliberate rejection of paths that might work on some long-path-enabled systems, in exchange for consistent behavior across the currently evidenced Windows/native-provider contract. The startup warning remains modal for any backup error; changing that UX is separate and not part of this root-cause correction.

**Resolution logic and reason.** Current normal `OrderFormSettings.Load` resolves `CRM_SETTINGS_PATH`, falling back through `CRMCompatibility.EnvironmentOverride` to `WEEKLY_LOGGER_SETTINGS_PATH`; with neither set, it chooses `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DeltaTrak\CRM\settings.json`. Only if that stable file is absent does it inspect `AppContext.BaseDirectory\settings.json` and import it into the stable path. It does **not** use `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DeltaTrak\WeeklyLogger\settings.json` as a settings source. A side-by-side Test22 stage has a different `AppContext.BaseDirectory` and deliberately contains no settings file, so it cannot automatically discover the adjacent settings in the separate Test20 folder.
